[79] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6208 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-10-28) — "Re: Plywood questions": full text and citation also at [construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md](/construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md#a-2009-recurrence-doubling-thinner-sheets-instead-of-a-single-thick-panel-and-marine-versus-ac-grade-from-a-working-shipwrights-experience); used the cheapest BS1088-certified marine ply he could source locally, never inspected since for aging, fully skinned in epoxy/glass and paint outside and epoxy/polystyrene inside; would use ACX from a home center only where the specified exterior grade couldn't be had, the design's over-engineering absorbing any slight void-related weakness. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[80] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6228 (openboat / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-14) — "Re: New England Paradox owners": full text and citation also at [boats/jill.md](/boats/jill.md#helping-a-prospective-new-england-builder-and-a-sandbar-story) and [construction/hull-assembly-sequence.md](/construction/hull-assembly-sequence.md#bulkhead-3-has-no-side-cleats-by-design); confirms none of his own nails went into ply end grain — all went into cleats — except bulkhead 3 (18mm), which has no side cleats; there he recalls using two nails per side as locating pins into pre-drilled holes. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[81] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6233 (openboat / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-16) — "Re: New England Paradox owners": full text and citation also at [construction/build-effort.md](/construction/build-effort.md#workspace) and [construction/fastening-and-gluing.md](/construction/fastening-and-gluing.md#why-nails-not-stitch-and-glue-a-weight-trade-off-paradox-doesnt-need); if building space is a problem, make all the small stuff (mast, spars, rudder) first while the space is still empty, ready to launch once the hull is finished; banging in a nail is easier and quicker than fiddling with ties and epoxy fillets, and saving weight with stitch-and-glue on a Paradox just means having to buy more lead to make up for it. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[82] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5441 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-08-28) — "Re: Steering Line Blocks": full text and citation also at [components/steering.md](/components/steering.md#where-the-corner-blocks-go-the-plans-against-practice); "I screwed mine to the intermediate deck beam." Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Al, Yeovil, England."
[83] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3923 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-08-27) — "Re: Side Motor Mount": full text and citation at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#further-field-experience-with-a-side-mounted-outboard); fitted an electric motor to "Little Jim"'s side rather than her transom, using brass hinges with a removable pin and a forward strut after an earlier bracket distorted at full throttle; cannot be left mounted while sailing; needs little angling to hold a straight course, turning to starboard quicker than to port. Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Al, Yeovil, England."
[84] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3950 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-09-03) — "Re: Side Motor Mount Photos, under IRONSIDES": full text and citation at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#a-worked-side-mount-installation-and-the-risk-of-shipping-water-while-fitting-it-afloat); calls Stephen Yahn's side mount an elegant solution, hoping it doesn't take on too much water while being fitted afloat. Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Al, Yeovil, England."
[85] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3952 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-09-04) — "Paradox outboard bracket": full text and citation at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#an-outboard-bracket-published-for-reference); cross-posted to `paradoxbuilders` and `smallsailboats`, has posted pictures of his own removable side-mounted outboard bracket on his website, noting the mount avoids sheet fouling for motor-sailing so long as heel does not submerge the outboard's head, and flagging a new web address after the old one was overwhelmed by spam. Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Al, Yeovil, England."
[86] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4751 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-11-09) — "Re: Vane gear": full text and citation at [operations/self-steering.md](/operations/self-steering.md#an-independent-vane-driven-auxiliary-rudder-and-a-bow-mounted-alternative); wonders whether a windvane could drive a small, independent rudder, applying only correcting adjustments, mounted on the foredeck instead of the stern, given the open space there; does not expect to fit one to "Little Jim." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[87] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5412 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-08-26) — "Re: Book Suggestion": full text and citation also at [operations/running-lights.md](/operations/running-lights.md#how-often-navigation-lights-actually-get-used-and-a-hand-torch-alternative); a plug-in masthead light would not be difficult to build; in the five years since launching, the tricolour has been switched on only once, believed unseen by anyone; the all-round white used occasionally at anchor, though a light hoisted temporarily in the topping lift would have done just as well; has much more faith in a powerful hand-held torch; notes it may be different where others sail. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[88] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) messages 3196/3197 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-10-21, "Re: Topping Lift"; 2005-10-21, "Re: Topping Lift"): full text and citation at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-second-later-topping-lift-question-and-two-more-confirmations). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[89] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5481 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-06) — "Re: Topping lift question": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#anticipating-an-upcoming-reef-leaving-the-topping-lift-set); the topping lift is normally slackened once sail is set, but sometimes left tight when a reef is expected soon — as sailing up a winding channel with the wind generally astern, reducing sail progressively corner by corner while the lift stays set throughout. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[90] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5631 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al Law — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-30) — "Re: Fitting the Sail": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md) and [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-tacking-direction-asymmetry-with-the-furl-direction); Sheet 9 shows the sail, and the furling line, coming to the port (outside) side of the boom; works ok for him; wonders whether the friction of the sail on the mast would be a problem if it were the other way round. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[91] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) messages 3835/3837 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-08-19; 2006-08-19) — "Re: Paradox sails": full text and citations at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#the-mk1mk2-tack-angle-difference-and-why-the-sail-cant-be-fenced-onto-the-drum) and [components/rig/index.md](/components/rig/index.md#the-reefing-triangle-and-why-reshaping-the-boom-taper-is-the-wrong-fix); explains the boom-taper/tack-angle control of the sail's furled position (a drum fence would only jam), reports never having the furling line come off the drum in three seasons, notes his own self-designed topping lift fouls up more often, corrects "taper of sail" to "taper of boom," and describes retrofitting his own under-tapered boom with a rigid-foam wedge taped at the clew end. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[92] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3876 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-08-19) — "Re: Paradox sails": gives his own Mk1 boom's worked taper dimensions — rectangular section with 8 mm radius corners, 50 x 38 mm at the clew, 35 x 38 mm at the tack, about 30 mm difference in circumference; notes the sail's forward creep while furling is not a problem so long as it is allowed for rather than fought. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[93] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3878 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-08-19) — "Re: Paradox sails": estimates the Mk2 sail's forward creep at about 2.5 in (citing Derek Clark's figure), well within the boom's length to stay clear of the furling reel once set up; reports furling is as easy as it looked in the WaterTribe video, apart from rope blisters until the hands harden. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[94] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) messages 3028/3029 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-08-03) — "Yard support" / "Re: Enuf's Calamity Update & the Yard": full text and citation at [components/rig/index.md](/components/rig/index.md#a-proposed-mast-mounted-traveller-to-keep-the-yard-close-when-reefed) and [boats/enuf.md](/boats/enuf.md#a-proposed-traveller-to-keep-the-yard-close-to-the-mast-when-reefed); the Welsford Houdini traveller idea, and a further cantilever/bamboo-crushing clarification for Derek Clark. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[95] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5636 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al Law — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-30) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#building-the-yuloh-itself-blade-curvature-and-the-pivot-boltsocket); confirms 3.5 in (90 mm) as the yuloh blade's widest point; when laminating the two planks together, puts a 30 mm block on the ground at each end and a weight in the middle. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[96] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5637 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al Law — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-01) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#reading-the-plans-a-first-time-builders-backlog-of-questions) and [boats/wizard-spawn.md](/boats/wizard-spawn.md#starting-the-yard-boom-and-yuloh-reading-drawing-12); the square on Drawing #12 is a section through the spar at that location — on the yard 700 mm from one end it is 42 x 38 mm with 12 mm radius corners, on the boom 1,850 mm from the tack it is 47 x 38 mm and 950 mm from the tack, 43 x 38 mm; on the yuloh, the flatter side sits on top when in use. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[97] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5641 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-01) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#building-the-yuloh-itself-blade-curvature-and-the-pivot-boltsocket) and [boats/wizard-spawn.md](/boats/wizard-spawn.md#starting-the-yard-boom-and-yuloh-reading-drawing-12); answering a hand-tools-only shaping question, describes bulk removal with a hand plane or coarse sanding disc in an angle grinder, then a strop of 1-inch sandpaper worked alternately over a corner for a smooth curve, checked against cardboard templates. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[98] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5640 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-01) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#blade-camber-the-ford-nomenclature-and-pivot-socket-dimensions); doesn't think it matters a great deal whether the blade is curved end to end or only at the ends; his own works fine either way; curving the full length makes it stow more neatly. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[99] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5646 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-02) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#blade-camber-the-ford-nomenclature-and-pivot-socket-dimensions); a 30 mm curve, however produced, just shifts the handle forward and down by about 30 mm, not a large change; part of his own reason for moving the pivot point nearer the handle was to stop the handle striking his face on the pull stroke. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[100] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5652 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-03) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#blade-camber-the-ford-nomenclature-and-pivot-socket-dimensions); the yuloh works like an aircraft wing or propeller blade, producing lift by its shape and angle of attack — "just like those awkward things, a keel or centre-board, that some sailors still insist on putting up with." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[101] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5655 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-03) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#an-alternative-sculling-oar-shape-the-vertical-scimitar-blade) and [operations/sculling-and-yuloh-technique.md](/operations/sculling-and-yuloh-technique.md#citations); doubts how a vertical oar would even work as propulsion rather than as a rudder; speed is not the only measure that matters, since energy efficiency counts too; restates driving his own boat at about 2 knots under yuloh — as fast as he can manage without quickly tiring, but sustainable all day. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[102] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5656 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-03) — "Re: Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#an-alternative-sculling-oar-shape-the-vertical-scimitar-blade); no illusion — low energy input, and a hand free to hold a can of something. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[103] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5632 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al Law — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-30) — "Little Jim, a non-standard Paradox": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#a-full-accounting-of-departures-from-the-stock-plans); a full written accounting of every departure from the stock plans, prompted by a beach conversation with a passerby who assumed "Little Jim" was built exactly to plan, organized as changes made during construction, changes made from experience, and untried ideas for a future build. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[104] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5672 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-05) — "Re: rudder blade": full text and citation also at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#rudder-ballast-without-a-lead-casting); confirms the leaded-rudder photos in the group's files section are Little Cruiser's, not Paradox's; recommends photocopying the aerofoil section to full size for cardboard templates, a square trailing edge for less drag and robustness, shaping the stock-bearing face flat and blending it into the foil, and leaving the uphaul-groove radius at full thickness; reports his own leading-edge/bottom wear fix — split copper water pipe hammered round the leading edge and bottom, bonded with epoxy — sound after three seasons aside from the paint wearing off, and notes a Duckworks-seen dacron-rope-in-epoxy-groove alternative. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[105] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5674 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-05) — "Re: rudder blade": full text and citation also at [construction/hull-assembly-sequence.md](/construction/hull-assembly-sequence.md#bulkhead-construction-details-not-spelled-out-on-the-plans); had wondered about the same "dado" term himself, and decided it is a recess the end of a divider fits into, similar to the mortice in a mortice-and-tenon joint. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[106] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5689 (OpenBoat / "" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-16) — "Re: Ironsides Floats": full text and citation also at [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#al-laws-reply-the-tiller-freedom-confirmed-a-fender-rigging-fix-and-an-open-question-about-sleeping-two); congratulates Stephen, seeing from his grin that he is pleased with the boat; confirms it is funny how people don't really believe the tiller-freedom effect until they've experienced it themselves; describes his own fender arrangement — eyes on the side of the cabin top, workable but requiring removal of the port fenders before hoisting sail; asks whether Stephen's partner is as happy, and whether the modifications will make it practical to sleep/live with two aboard. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[107] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5693 (yahns / "yahn101a" / Stephen Yahn, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-16) — "Re: Ironsides Floats": full text and citation also at [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#answering-al-law-a-partners-first-impression-and-the-sleeping-two-question-left-for-another-day) and [people/stephen-yahn.md](/people/stephen-yahn.md#citations); thanks Al for the rudder-control advice, now experienced firsthand; his wife found the boat surprisingly tiny outside and roomy inside; defers judging whether the widened bulkhead 3 suits sleeping two, since his own snoring seems the likelier obstacle; will fit the cabin-top fender eyes Al described. Signed "Steve Yahn."
[108] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3672 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-05-25) — "Re: Topping Lift Again": full text and citation at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-third-topping-lift-question-reading-the-deck-tube-layout-at-the-mast). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[109] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3675 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-05-26) — "Re: Deck Chewing": full text and citation at [components/rig/index.md](/components/rig/index.md#tack-pipe-deck-wear). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[110] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3681 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-05-26) — "Re: Deck Chewing": full text and citation at [components/rig/index.md](/components/rig/index.md#why-the-mainsheet-presented-at-the-helmsmans-back-is-not-awkward-in-practice). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[111] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3740 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-07-08) — "Re: Deck Loops": full text and citation at [components/deck.md](/components/deck.md#deck-loop-material-choices-and-a-fastening-variant). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[112] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3741 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-07-08) — "Re: Spar storage": full text and citation at [components/deck.md](/components/deck.md#storing-the-mast-boom-and-sail-when-not-sailing). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[113] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3841 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-08-10) — "Re: Tiller Shape": full text and citation at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#tiller-construction-laminated-wood-versus-bent-metal-tube); banged some nails into a board and laminated the tiller round them. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[114] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3859 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-08-15) — "Re: Rudder": his own pintles are on the centerline, putting the rudder blade slightly to starboard and the tiller slightly to port. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[115] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4984 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-13) — "Re: Hello :o) In new here.": full text and citation also at [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#the-rear-deck-entry-question-resolved-attachment-point-not-entry-point-is-the-real-hazard); had considered a deck-entry sheet after noticing Little Cruiser's, but decided he didn't know enough about the downward pull's effect on the sail's set, there being no kicking strap; the real fix for a fouled motor is bringing the boom's end further inboard or freeing the sheet from the boom's tip, for which a claw fitting running loose on the boom (needing the longitudinal bridle wire to position it) might work; gave the question up at the design stage and found, once built, that not needing a motor at all made the fouling a non-issue for him. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[116] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4988 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-16) — "Re: Hello :o) In new here.": full text and citation also at [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#the-rear-deck-entry-question-resolved-attachment-point-not-entry-point-is-the-real-hazard); a linked photo didn't work; restates that the fouling problem is the boom-end attachment, not the sheet's entry point; recounts an unplanned gybe where the loose sheet wrapped around a cleat or the yuloh pin, easily freed by hand but which would be much harder to clear from a mounted motor, risking tearing it off the bracket; taking in all slack before gybing may avoid the problem, but demands more concentration than he gives it. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[117] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5001 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-18) — "Re: Sheeting arrangements": full text and citation also at [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#a-pushpit-as-a-further-fouling-remedy-and-self-steering-vane-gear-clearance-confirmed); asks whether the Mk2 sail carries a shorter boom than his own Mk1; suggests a pushpit to keep the sheet clear of a mounted motor; on self-steering vane-gear fouling, judges the part most likely to tangle sits aft of the boom's own end, out of the sheet's reach. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[118] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5157 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-10) — "Re: Mast electrical wires": full text and citation also at [construction/electrical-wiring.md](/construction/electrical-wiring.md#a-2008-recurrence-resolved-above-deck-through-the-vent-box); fits a waterproof connector at the mast's foot, feeding the lead through the vent from inside; warns a below-deck side exit makes stepping/unstepping the mast harder, recommending an above-deck exit if a side route is wanted. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[119] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5165 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-12) — "Re: Mast Making": full text and citation also at [chine-runners/construction.md](/chine-runners/construction.md#the-inboard-fillet-an-aerodynamic-case-for-omitting-it-versus-a-turbulence-caution); agrees a fellow builder built to plan; expects it will be interesting to see the un-filleted runner's performance, since a temporary fillet could easily be retrofitted for comparison; predicts a lot of turbulence in the corner without it. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[120] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5217 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-22) — "Re: Boom spliting": full text and citation also at [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#boom-end-splitting-at-the-mainsheet-tang-bolt-and-a-construction-fix) and [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#the-2008-scilly-isles-cruise-little-jims-side-of-the-log); drilled a larger hole, filled it with epoxy and fibres, then drilled and tapped it for the 8 mm bolt used, no problems since; agrees with Andrew Graham's rounded-boom-end idea, having fitted his own 10 mm spacer for the same effect; on the recent trip with Bill Serjeant, was impressed by how much the boom and yard bent sailing hard. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[121] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5222 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-22) — "Re: Boom spliting": full text and citation also at [operations/sculling-and-yuloh-technique.md](/operations/sculling-and-yuloh-technique.md#matt-laydens-motors-are-a-luxury-philosophy-put-into-practice); had a large box of stainless 8 mm bolts on hand, so tended to use 8 mm throughout; without the outboard on the Scilly crossing there would have been a lot more waiting for the right wind — "if you have a timetable you really do need a motor" — calling it "really a psychological barrier," having made longer coastal trips without a motor without a second thought sailing alone. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[122] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5224 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-23) — "Re: Storing gas": full text and citation also at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#a-side-mounted-motor-and-fuel-sponson-considered-and-rejected-a-three-position-transom-bracket); the same spot suits an electric outboard for fouling clearance, but cannot stay there for real sailing unless waterproofed, since the lee rail runs close to submerged on a fast reach; a stability-curve heel of about 20 degrees is normal to windward in a Force 3; fuel, if carried, is better kept on the aft deck against the cabin window, clear of sailing gear and waves. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[123] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5448 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-04) — "Re: Sailrite kits": full text and citation also at [components/rig/sail.md](/components/rig/sail.md#buying-vs-building-a-sail-recommendations-and-firsthand-accounts); "Stick 'Sailrite' into Google." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[124] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5457 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-04) — "Re: Force 8 gale": full text and citation also at [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#clarifying-the-offshore-spares-list-the-tack-arrangement-spacer-and-the-furling-axles-field-record) and [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-bearing-fix-for-the-furling-axles-forward-thrust); confirms his only failure was on the tack-strut spacer, built too cheaply without fully appreciating the loads it had to transmit, and it bent; the boom's own axle is in practice held in place by gravity rather than its retaining screw, often left loose on day or weekend sails with no ill effect; domed the axle bar's end, brazed a cap over a length of water pipe, and bonded it into the boom cap-end first, so the axle rides a rattling-loose fit against the cap — with a smear of grease it has operated perfectly ever since; judges a sealed bearing would be a retrograde step, easier for something to go wrong and much harder to fix when it does. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[125] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5461 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-05) — "Re: Force 8 gale": full text and citation also at [components/rig/tack-strop.md](/components/rig/tack-strop.md#the-tack-strut-also-carries-compression-not-just-tension) and [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#boom-end-splitting-at-the-mainsheet-tang-bolt-and-a-construction-fix); restates for a new correspondent that his spacer pipe, once collapsed, is now water pipe capped with a stainless steel U-piece, the plans' Schedule 40 brass pipe still being hard to find in the UK; describes the tang assembly again — boom end drilled, filled with epoxy, drilled and tapped for an 8mm bolt, made good and beefy against the mainsheet's forward-pulling bending load. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[126] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5475 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-06) — "Re: Topping Lift": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-second-use-for-the-topping-lift-readiness-yuloh-clearance-and-a-rain-awning) and [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#an-umbrella-instead-of-a-topping-lift-awning); has an umbrella that clips onto the hatch, good for sun or rain, though rarely gets the chance to use it for sun. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[127] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6109 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-21) — "Re: Vane Gear and Anchoring": full text and citation also at [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#a-first-cruise-four-days-in-south-puget-sound-july-2009); also finds his own umbrella clamp invaluable, "but it has little to do with the sun." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[128] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6109 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-21) — "Re: Vane Gear and Anchoring": full text and citation also at [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#a-first-cruise-four-days-in-south-puget-sound-july-2009); has a couple of 1-inch holes in his own sole hatches that make them easy to lift. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[129] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6107 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-20) — "Re: Don Elliott's 'Paradox Builders Guide'": full text and citation also at [people/don-elliott.md](/people/don-elliott.md#after-his-death) and [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#a-july-2009-recurrence-still-unreachable-through-don-elliotts-own-address); as no one else had answered, offers his own copy of the Building Manual, conditional on the newcomer first getting clearance from Don's family. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[130] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5627 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al Law — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-30) — "Re: Fitting the Sail": full text and citation also at [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#a-grommetyard-mismatch-resolved-and-the-sails-first-hoist) and [construction/sailmaking.md](/construction/sailmaking.md#a-grommetyard-lead-angle-mismatch-and-two-independent-fixes); had the same grommet-position problem on his own sail, so it may simply be correct to drawing rather than a kit fault; solved it by adding two more grommets in the side of the sleeve. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[131] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6102 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-15) — "Re: About the topping lift.": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#the-question-recurs-once-more-in-2009); no, it is not necessary, since Matt doesn't use one — but would not like to be without it himself. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[132] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6116 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-23) — "Re: About Topping Lift": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#the-question-recurs-once-more-in-2009); describes his own topping-lift design — rope at each end of the boom joining and passing through an eye at the masthead, holding the boom's hoisted position — and points to photos in the "Little Jim" Photos-section folder; the only disadvantage is care needed not to get the yard on the wrong side of it when starting to hoist. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[133] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6118 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-23) — "Re: About Topping Lift": full text and citation also at [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#a-2009-recurrence-across-four-boats-and-two-new-fix-ideas); has no outboard fitted to Little Jim and can see no way to mount one clear of the ropes; those who do carry one tend to lower sail and start the motor as soon as tacking is needed; carries an electric trolling motor instead, stowed inboard on a bracket alongside the seat, clear of the ropes in flat water though its propeller lifts clear once the boat heels; finds the yuloh generally sufficient. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[134] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6128 (OpenBoat / "" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-07-24) — "Re: About Topping Lift": full text and citation also at [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#a-2009-recurrence-across-four-boats-and-two-new-fix-ideas); replying to Jimmy Lu's junk-sail suggestion, notes a doubled mainsheet would still have to be set well aft to get the centre of pressure in the right place. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[135] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6194 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-10-23) — "Re: tack strop space tube": full text and citation also at [components/rig/tack-strop.md](/components/rig/tack-strop.md#a-may-1994-plans-revision-explains-the-forked-vs-plain-spacer-tube); at the top of Sheet 9 there is a side and end view of the strut assembly — the spindle (12mm brass rod) bends down and splits, joined by a 6mm stainless steel bolt and peened nut to the split top of the strut tube; the tack strop is fed through the tube and its knot nestles in the "Y," stopping it pulling back through; the assembly needs to be fairly sturdy, since it sees significant compressive and bending loads sailing reefed. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[136] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6197 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-10-23) — "Re: tack strop space tube": full text and citation also at [components/rig/tack-strop.md](/components/rig/tack-strop.md#a-may-1994-plans-revision-explains-the-forked-vs-plain-spacer-tube); confirms he was working from a plan set predating the May 1994 revision — the older, unforked joint collapses sideways under load when sailing reefed, exactly as his own tack strut did before being rebuilt. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[137] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6199 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-10-27) — "Re: Plywood questions": full text and citation also at [construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md](/construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md#a-2009-recurrence-doubling-thinner-sheets-instead-of-a-single-thick-panel-and-marine-versus-ac-grade-from-a-working-shipwrights-experience); doubling thinner sheets is worth it only if the specified thickness can't be sourced locally, since gluing two layers void-free needs a lot of epoxy; used the cheapest marine grade available with no bending trouble — sides bent with two fingers, the bottom pulled down by nailing bow-to-stern with the stern propped up and a helper's weight added for the final stretch; the design is already far stronger than needed, "not sailing on the same sea as me" if it seems otherwise; the chine logs are the one part the plans do call for laminating from two pieces, where a full-length piece can't be steamed. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[138] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6255 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-26) — "Re: Roller reefing question": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#why-the-forward-reefing-fitting-needs-so-many-parts-tension-when-hoisted-compression-when-reefed); once thought he could out-guess Matt and simplified the forward reefing fitting himself, but a test hoist showed the change would not work, so rebuilt to plan, which has worked perfectly since; explains the tack fixing is in tension when full sail is up (the lower strut then superfluous) but comes into compression once reefed, subject to interesting bending loads as the boom swings round the mast; the fitting also serves as an essential part of the sail-stowage system; advises not fiddling with something that works well. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[139] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6266 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-01) — "Re: Roller reefing question": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#the-y-fittings-purpose-confirmed-and-the-plans-own-may-1996-revision); corrects a fellow correspondent directly — the Y split is necessary, since with the original set-up, sailing reefed with the strut under compression, the joint collapses sideways and the boom drops onto the cabin top or, if running, onto the deck, making it awkward to tack or gybe "(and can give you a headache)." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[140] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6276 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-01) — "Re: Roller reefing question": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-follow-up-what-removing-the-tack-strop-entirely-would-demonstrate); clarifies a tack-strop-removal thought experiment was illustrative only, not a real proposal; confirms both Matt Layden's original fitting and his own independent simplification of it collapse sideways in compression once reefed; mentions he is Scottish by nationality despite his standing "Yeovil, England" signature. Signed "Al."
[141] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5760 (openboat / "" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-27) — "Re: Ironsides Floats": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#little-jims-actual-trailered-weight-and-two-contrasting-uk-trailer-setups) and [boats/johanna.md](/boats/johanna.md#two-2008-questions-answered-an-outboard-bracket-and-little-jims-actual-trailered-weight); his own trailer is built as near as possible to the boat's own size, with the drawbar sticking out the front and the wheels sticking out the side; supposes it could be made narrower by raising the boat and putting the wheels underneath. Sent via a Pegasus Mail client through a Plusnet relay, `From:` display name blank. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[142] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5753 (OpenBoat / "" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-26) — "Re: Ironsides Floats": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#little-jims-actual-trailered-weight-and-two-contrasting-uk-trailer-setups) and [boats/johanna.md](/boats/johanna.md#two-2008-questions-answered-an-outboard-bracket-and-little-jims-actual-trailered-weight); at the last weighing the boat was 526 kg on the trailer, loaded with stores; had a trailer specially built that doesn't require the wheels to go in the water, which makes it sturdy and weighing in at just over 400 kg, so well over 900 kg all up; keeping the all-up weight below 750 kg may be legal without brakes, but manhandling on a slope would be a problem, and would have fitted brakes regardless since his own drive is on a slope. Sent via a Pegasus Mail client through a Plusnet relay, `From:` display name blank. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[143] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3490 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-03-23) — "Re: RE TRAPPED IN BOAT": Paradox is self-righting without any human action, theoretically from 160 degrees; a 90-degree knockdown, put down to a seamanship error, had recovered itself before he had registered what had happened — in this respect the boat is far more a yacht than a dinghy. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[144] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3491 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-03-23) — "Re: I am concerned that a sailor is trapped in the yacht.": full text and citation at [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#whether-a-closed-cabin-is-a-safety-risk-or-a-comfort). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[145] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 2130 (openboat / "Alastair", `paradoxbuilders`, 2004-06-02) — "Re: new sail rev.": full text and citation at [operations/sailing-characteristics.md](/operations/sailing-characteristics.md#center-of-effort-rudder-stall-and-telltale-placement-as-the-real-tacking-variables).
[146] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4028 (william / "barnacleid" / Bill Serjeant, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-10-05) — "Re: Haliyard and Reef lines": full text and citation at [boats/faith.md](/boats/faith.md#a-trim-question-and-little-jims-tacking-quirk); asks Alastair how much fore-and-aft trim actually matters, having assumed Paradox was heavy enough that the ballast sitting slightly one way or the other would make no real difference. Signed "Bill."
[147] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4030 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-10-05) — "Re: Haliyard and Reef lines": full text and citation at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#sudden-total-loss-of-rudder-effect-mid-tack-and-tacking-technique); on launching "Little Jim" he occasionally had trouble tacking, not coming up to the wind or getting stuck head to wind; playing with the trim mostly sorted it, though it may just have been his own developing technique and not the boat; sails happily on any point regardless of trim, tested with a passenger's weight moved about; only tacking seems trim-sensitive; gybes benignly, always giving another way round. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[148] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4031 (william / "barnacleid" / Bill Serjeant, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-10-05) — "Re: Haliyard and Reef lines": full text and citation at [boats/faith.md](/boats/faith.md#a-trim-question-and-little-jims-tacking-quirk); looks forward to discovering whether "Faith" shows the same tacking characteristic once she is on the water. Signed "Bill."
[149] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4102 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-11-11) — "Re: Great Lugsail Article": full text and citation at [components/rig/index.md](/components/rig/index.md#balanced-lug-standing-lug-or-dipping-lug); asks whether Paradox carries a standing, balanced, or dipping lug, reasoning through why the tack's forward-of-mast position without ever dipping rules out a true dipping lug, and why the boom not fastening at the mast rules out a strict balanced lug. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[150] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4853 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-01-22) — "Re: Considering Dingos and Modifying Paradox": full text and citation also at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#reference-sailor-heights-and-weights-and-a-two-up-alternative-the-bolger-micro); gives his own reference figures — 5'6" and 180 lb — Paradox fitting him "like a glove," though squeezing right to the fit's limit is best done in quiet conditions. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[151] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6273 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-01) — "Re: Tall/large people in Paradox": full text and citation also at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#a-further-heightweight-question-answered-december-2009); doubts height alone would keep a newcomer from fitting; if the sleeping space is too short, the opening through to the forward storage can be modified level with the cabin sole so the feet stick through (a mod he made to his own boat for other reasons); suppleness matters more than size, and no owner has ever complained the cabin is too cramped; gives his own figures, 5'6" and 180 lb, finding it "quite roomy." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[152] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4694 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-08-30) — "Comfy seating": full text and citation at [components/interior.md](/components/interior.md#a-flip-up-backrest-at-bulkhead-4); describes a cut-out in the seat support forward of bulkhead 4 that lets the seat flip up into a raked backrest. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[153] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4858 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-01-23) — "Re: Seating Position in Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/interior.md](/components/interior.md#seating-position-back-comfort-and-a-mini-pilothouse-idea); sitting very straight in "Little Jim," his hair just brushes the hatch cover at about 35.5 in, being 5'6" tall; recommends lowering the sole over raising the roof for more clearance, since raising the roof has knock-on effects on sail stowage; notes that although Derek considered a number of possibilities, he ended up building "Enuf" approximately to plan; remains pleased with using the seat as a backrest when sitting inside, and wonders about adding a couple of armrests to its base; floats an untried small inflatable pool chair as "real armchair sailing." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[154] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4974 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-10) — "Re: Hello :o) In new here.": full text and citation also at [similar-boat-designs/alternatives-for-a-larger-sailor.md](/similar-boat-designs/alternatives-for-a-larger-sailor.md#a-counterpoint-paradox-as-arguably-too-seaworthy-for-two-up-sailing-and-a-further-design-pointer); suggests Paradox's real problem may be being too seaworthy; describes a family motorboat tolerant of rough water but prone to rolling past force 4, kept off open water in poor conditions as a comfort rather than safety matter; has himself been tempted out in conditions that would not have been sensible sailing two-up; five years without being genuinely caught out by a sudden weather change suggests adequate forecast-reading rather than luck. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[155] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4977 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-10) — "Re: Hello :o) In new here.": full text and citation also at [similar-boat-designs/alternatives-for-a-larger-sailor.md](/similar-boat-designs/alternatives-for-a-larger-sailor.md#a-counterpoint-paradox-as-arguably-too-seaworthy-for-two-up-sailing-and-a-further-design-pointer); confirms a storm-force Solent crossing came from Poole while two 40 ft yachts struggled across from Lymington, one turning back; judges it "one case where I would not have sailed if I had someone else aboard." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[156] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5458 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-04) — "Re: Sailrite kits": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#a-partial-reef-account-in-a-force-5-7-and-the-hulls-way-after-masting-down-september-2008) and [components/rig/sail.md](/components/rig/sail.md#choosing-between-the-two-2008-sailrite-listings); a couple of weeks earlier, sailing in a forecast Force 5-7 with about 2 ft of luff unrolled, was still doing over 5 knots; lowering the mast to pass under a bridge, was still doing 2 knots. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[157] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5478 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-06) — "Re: Sailrite kits": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#a-partial-reef-account-in-a-force-5-7-and-the-hulls-way-after-masting-down-september-2008); downplays how exciting the sail actually was — over-canvassed at first, settled once reduced; gives the passage in full, Calshot to Langstone Harbour under the Hayling Island bridge into Chichester Harbour, timed near low water for calmer conditions. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[158] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5710 (OpenBoat / "" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-20) — "Re: Ironsides Floats": full text and citation also at [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#sourcing-ballast-on-a-budget-concrete-bricks-against-scarce-pricier-lead) and [design/weight-and-ballast.md](/design/weight-and-ballast.md#ballast-material-choice-lead-against-concrete-iron-and-other-alternatives); replying to Ron Church, suggests spending ballast money on something with a second purpose — a dismantleable storm anchor or a good battery; reports his own lead now down to about 36 kg (75 lb) after weighing "Little Jim" afresh, the bicycle having been the last big addition; expects to think twice before adding more gear. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[159] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5737 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-23) — "Re: Silly Gadgets": full text and citation also at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#commercial-and-diy-pedal-generators-and-a-real-number-verdict-against-the-whole-idea); replying to Nels's 60-watt/5-amp pedal generator, reports his own small Minn Kota outboard pushes "Little Jim" at 2 knots consuming 15 amps on its most efficient setting (3 knots for 30 amps on the higher setting) — so pedaling three hours would be needed for every hour of motoring; if spending more on the system at all, would settle instead for a big solar panel and/or wind generator and a big battery bank; as it is, charges the 80AH battery before setting off and uses the motor only in extreme need. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[160] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6086 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-06-10) — "Paradox self righting": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#beale-park-thames-boat-show-and-a-righting-moment-measurement-june-2009) and [design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md](/design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md#firsthand-knockdown-accounts); "Little Jim" was at the Beale Park Thames Boat Show the previous weekend as part of the Dinghy Cruising Association display; pulled the boat over to measure her righting moment — cruising trim, sail hoisted, a body at the helm position, mast pulled down until the water was a couple of inches below the hatch, a 25 lb spring balance at the masthead reading "well over 25 pounds," so no accurate value obtained; confirms a correctly ballasted Paradox definitely self-rights from 90 degrees, and notes the amount of rolling before she finally settled; links a YouTube video of the exercise. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[161] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6241 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-21) — "Re: Batteries as ballast": full text and citation also at [design/weight-and-ballast.md](/design/weight-and-ballast.md#a-2009-revisit-an-in-service-battery-and-sizing-figures); has fitted an 80Ah AGM battery (about 12 kg) forward of bulkhead 3, in the gap between the water tank and the side; drives a small trolling motor good for about 8 miles at 2 knots, motor normally stowed inboard (mind proximity to the compass); uses the yuloh for short distances, glad of the motor on the rare occasions it is needed; also charges his other electronics. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[162] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6247 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-23) — "Re: Batteries as ballast": full text and citation also at [design/weight-and-ballast.md](/design/weight-and-ballast.md#a-2009-revisit-an-in-service-battery-and-sizing-figures); cannot weigh the battery again to check, but recalls it replacing one of his own 12 kg (25 lb) lead ballast bricks; comparing the relative size of the solid lead and the battery, the figure looks about right. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[163] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6256 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-26) — "Re: BALLAST": full text and citation also at [boats/cachivache.md](/boats/cachivache.md#ballast-railroad-track-on-hand); advises starting railroad-track ballast sections close to bulkhead 3 without fixing them down permanently, since some pieces will likely need moving to get the trim right on first test sails; better too much ballast than too little. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[164] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5741 (openboat / "alopenboat" / "Alastair", `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-25) — "Re: Practical Gadgeteering": full text and citation also at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#omitting-the-boarding-step-for-an-outboards-sake-and-a-harder-lesson-on-reboarding) and [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#a-propeller-mock-up-on-ironsides-and-the-draft-trade-off-it-raised); not for shallow water then, and asks what would be done with a propeller mock-up once stowed; separately asks why the boarding step was left off the rudder, finding it useful when setting off from a beach. `From:` display name reads "Alastair" on this message, where this account has shown an empty display name for roughly fifteen batches (and shows empty again the same batch, in citation [165] below). Unsigned.
[165] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5743 (OpenBoat / "" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-10-25) — "Re: Practical Gadgeteering": full text and citation also at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#omitting-the-boarding-step-for-an-outboards-sake-and-a-harder-lesson-on-reboarding), [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#a-transom-slap-noise-at-anchor-explained-by-trim), and [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#a-propeller-mock-up-on-ironsides-and-the-draft-trade-off-it-raised); doesn't know for certain, but recalls hearing the hull would ground before the outboard, putting it at under 9 in; explains the "plunging sound" as the transom bottom sitting proud of the water so ripples slap the hull, which stopped once the boat was loaded to sit deeper, the boarding step now staying fully submerged; doubts the step's drag is measurable at 2 knots. Sent via a Pegasus Mail client through a Plusnet relay, `From:` display name blank. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[166] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6215 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-11) — "Re: building material for paradox": full text and citation also at [design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md](/design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md#firsthand-knockdown-accounts) and [boats/bris-paradox.md](/boats/bris-paradox.md#a-2009-revisit-still-the-groups-standing-answer); Paradox is a coastal cruiser, not Yrvind's ocean crosser, so Yrvind's costlier foam-composite approach isn't needed — already extremely rugged and lined with foam giving buoyancy enough not to sink even full of water; in six years of sailing, water has come aboard beyond a splash or spray only once, when the mast dipped into the water and about two litres came below. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[167] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6216 (mwatersworld / "mwatersworld" / Mark Waters, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-11) — "Re: building material for paradox": curious about the mast-in-the-water incident, asks the circumstances and conditions, whether the hatch was open or closed, and what lessons were learned. Signed "Mark."
[168] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6217 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-11-11) — "Re: building material for paradox": answers Mark Waters — points to his own website's "Trip 4" page (`www.little.jim.freeuk.com`) and a YouTube video (`youtube.com/user/alopenboat`) documenting the incident; lessons learned are the usual ones, reef early and watch the weather, but the experience gave him confidence the boat will look after him even when he does something silly. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[169] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5189 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-18) — "Re: Ironsides progress": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#the-construction-site-draws-further-thanks-june-2008); corrects a thank-you from a fellow builder — the website took minimal work, its design "snitched" from someone else, with only the photo file names needing to be typed in correctly; glad it proved helpful regardless. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[170] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 1335 (openboat / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2003-11-16) — "Re: Square tubs verses round tubs": wry closing remark on the Don/Glen exchange — advice from Don, "which apparently isn't his personal opinion," and advice from Glen, "with supporting reasoning, which presumably is his personal opinion," leaving everyone free to form their own view without it becoming "another of Don's confrontations."
[171] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4470 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-05-18) — "Re: Portsmouth Yardstick": full text and citation at [operations/sailing-characteristics.md](/operations/sailing-characteristics.md#no-portsmouth-yardstick-rating-on-record); declines to help — competitive sailing contradicts most of his own reasons for going sailing, since Paradox is "a comfortable home"; quotes the Water Rat's "there is nothing... half so much worth doing as simply messing about in boats" passage from Kenneth Grahame's *The Wind in the Willows* in full. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[172] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4174 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-01-09) — "Re: Why not scale?": full text and citation at [design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md](/design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md#why-not-scale-moment-of-inertia-and-getting-matts-plans-directly); mass scales predictably but the moment of inertia does not, so a scaled-up hull may float like the original yet handle quite differently underway; the smaller the increase, the closer the behavior stays to the original. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[173] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4179 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-01-16) — "Re: 150% Paradox Rendered": full text and citation at [design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md](/design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md#a-150-suv-render-and-doubts-about-handling-at-that-size); doubts the 150%-scale render would handle like its smaller sibling; jokes about the render being full of dogs. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[174] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4186 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-01-19) — "Re: Why not scale?": full text and citation at [design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md](/design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md#a-newcomers-case-for-a-commissioned-larger-design-and-a-stock-scale-answer); wonders how many actual Paradox owners, as opposed to non-owners, would really want the boat bigger — "not me." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[175] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 2822 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-06-11) — "Re: Scale mockup of wind genny and radar on Paradox": full text and citation at [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#a-wind-against-tide-encounter-and-the-case-against-foredeck-storage); a PVC-tape sheer line washed off and wrapped round the mast and cabin, yet not a drop of water got inside.
[176] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 2823 (Chris / "wedgesail" / Chris Ostlind, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-06-11) — "Re: Stuff on deck of Paradox": asks Alastair whether the dry-inside result was down to design or workmanship.
[177] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 2826 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-06-12) — "Re: Stuff on deck of Paradox": full text and citation at [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#a-wind-against-tide-encounter-and-the-case-against-foredeck-storage); credits the design, not workmanship, singling out the cabin hatch seal.
[178] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 2962 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-07-21) — "Re: Mainsheet and outboard": full text and citation at [merits/inland-waterway-and-canal-touring.md](/merits/inland-waterway-and-canal-touring.md#the-yulohs-real-limit-fine-for-a-lock-or-a-marina-not-for-windward-distance); the yuloh's windward limitation, replying to Martin Feaviour's canal-touring question.
[179] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 2967 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-07-21) — "Re: please help!!!": tells an impatient newcomer that Paradox may not suit him and repeats the `microcruising.com` plans source. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[180] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3177 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2005-09-11) — "Re: study plan questions": full text and citation at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#glen-maxwells-measured-cabin-dimensions). [181] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3388 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-02-25) — "Re: Paradox max pilot weight,height": full text and citation at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#pilot-weight-height-and-stability-at-stock-length); weight is irrelevant given removable lead ballast; gives sitting headroom (860 mm) and stock/extended sleeping length (1.8 m/+0.8 m). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."

[192] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3307 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-01-22) — "Re: Paradox questions": answers a newcomer weighing a Paradox against a Little Cruiser: about 18 months to build, with build pictures on his own site, and a flat sleeping area 1.9 m by 0.9 m that a small modification to the forward access would extend by roughly another 0.5 m of legroom. [193] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3314 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-01-23) — "Re: Paradox questions": full text and citation at [operations/first-boat-suitability.md](/operations/first-boat-suitability.md#the-two-person-question-answered-directly).
