[194] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3317 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-01-23) — "Re: Paradox questions": full text and citation at [operations/first-boat-suitability.md](/operations/first-boat-suitability.md#the-two-person-question-answered-directly) and [operations/first-boat-suitability.md](/operations/first-boat-suitability.md#build-time-figures-and-difficulty-rating). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[195] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) messages 3362/3365 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-02-27) — "Re: Paradox max pilot weight,height": full text and citation at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#pilot-weight-height-and-stability-at-stock-length); disputes the stability caution, pointing to his own "Stability" webpage and photo trying to capsize "Little Jim" by hand; cites Matt Layden's own two-up sailing and Hugo Vihlen's "Father's Day" (105 days in a 5 ft 4 in boat). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[196] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3401 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-02-28) — "Re: Paradox max pilot weight,height": full text and citation at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#pilot-weight-height-and-stability-at-stock-length); encourages Paul Reppeto to pursue his "Big Paradox" idea and report the result. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[197] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3585 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-04-12) — "Re: Spar and Sail Internal Stowage": full text and citation at [construction/transom-deck-plate.md](/construction/transom-deck-plate.md#stowing-the-mast-boom-yard-and-sail-through-the-transom-hatch-for-road-travel). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[198] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3590 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-04-13) — "Re: Enuf pics?": full text and citation at [boats/enuf.md](/boats/enuf.md#a-carpal-tunnel-operation-halts-progress-and-a-five-week-pause); has slept aboard his own motor cruiser this way and would consider it in a Paradox; cautions against remote laybys, recommending a residential road instead. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[199] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3604 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-04-14) — "Re: Enuf pics?": invites Jim Hall to a DCA meeting at Hurst Point (29-30 April) with a test sail at Keyhaven; blames a "racing lobby" marketing effect for unconventional designs' poor sales; has written off his own build cost. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[200] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3605 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-04-14) — "Re: Enuf pics?": gives the "Little Jim" build's cost/time figures — about 18 months, roughly £2,500. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[201] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3609 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-04-14) — "Re: Enuf pics?": full text and citation at [boats/enuf.md](/boats/enuf.md#a-recurring-close-of-the-enuf-pics-thread); jokes "the water goes round the other way in Oz" in reply to Derek Clark's windward-conditions question. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[202] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3747 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-07-15) — "Re: Paradox Plans": full text and citation at [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#a-july-2006-recurrence-and-a-blunt-assessment-of-the-paid-manuals-quality). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[203] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3752 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-07-16) — "Re: Bulkhead 3": full text and citation at [components/bulkheads.md](/components/bulkheads.md#modifying-bulkhead-3-to-gain-room-for-sleeping-two). Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[204] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3982 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-09-12) — "Re: Faith's rudder": full text and citation at [boats/faith.md](/boats/faith.md#faiths-rudder-a-sheet-fairlead-fix-and-boom-tang-strength); praises Bill's rudder photos; describes the mainsheet/pintle fouling problem and his wooden-insert fairlead fix. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[205] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 3998 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2006-09-17) — "Re: Goodbye": full text and citation at [boats/enuf.md](/boats/enuf.md#a-departure-an-outpouring-of-support-and-second-thoughts); "No need to leave Derek, in fact you have a duty to stay. You contain 11% of the complete build information in the known world." Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[206] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4466 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-05-18) — "Paradox water ballast, or not!": full text and citation at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#abandoning-water-ballast-for-lead-after-a-leaking-tank-inspection-cover-may-2007); a leaking ballast-tank inspection cover after a rough F5-6 weekend prompted abandoning water ballast for permanent lead, keeping 10 kg as water ballast in 2-litre bottles for trim. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[207] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4697 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-08-30) — "Re: Scaling and modifying paradox.": full text and citation at [design/blue-water-suitability.md](/design/blue-water-suitability.md#a-round-britain-question-and-whether-paradox-is-a-dinghy-or-a-micro-yacht); reports the other boat's cramped accommodation secondhand; judges Paradox "not really the boat for those who enjoy sailing on the edge," since most of the time she sails herself with the skipper along for the ride. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[208] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4693 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-08-30) — "Re: Scaling and modifying paradox.": full text and citation at [design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md](/design/ideas/stretched-paradox.md#a-newcomers-own-needs-redesign-and-a-competing-proportional-stretch-approach); tells a newcomer to get the plans first, so he knows where he is really starting from. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[209] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4703 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2007-09-05) — "Re: Chine runners": full text and citation at [chine-runners/construction.md](/chine-runners/construction.md#fitting-order-blank-before-or-after-the-bottom-panel); agrees the before-bottom fitting order will be much easier to clamp, especially if the blank is steamed first; recalls finding it impossible to bend full-length runner pieces into position on his own build, cutting them into ~1 ft lengths instead. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[210] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4902 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-01-28) — "Re: Cabin Sole.": full text and citation also at [boats/faith.md](/boats/faith.md#drying-out-on-a-sunken-mooring-block-during-the-west-country-cruise) and [design/hull-scantlings.md](/design/hull-scantlings.md#citations); Paradox wins out over both NOLI and Enigma in the "comfort of confidence" of never needing to survey a drying-out spot for rocks, the thick bottom board being more than strong enough regardless; recounts Faith drying out on a submerged concrete mooring sinker during the West Country cruise, with no damage found once refloated. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[211] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4906 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-01-28) — "Re: Cabin Sole.": full text and citation also at [design/hull-scantlings.md](/design/hull-scantlings.md#citations); jokes that a lighter hull will be fine in the Solent's soft mud; recalls two other boats seen the previous year that had sustained damage severe enough to be taking on water, one from drying out on a stone and one from striking a just-submerged post, and a further grounding the year before that forced a cruise to be abandoned and the boat beached immediately. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[212] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4912 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-01-29) — "Re: Cabin Sole.": full text and citation also at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#beaching-stern-first-a-sleeping-comfort-technique-and-why-it-doesnt-damage-the-rudder); has done the same; an upward force on the rudder, as when beaching, pushes it up until it hits the underside of the boat rather than loading the pintles (the lower pintle is 15 mm regardless), so bouncing on a beach while drying out or refloating, though it sounds and can feel bad, does no harm to the structure. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[213] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4914 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-01-30) — "Re: Rudder blade stop": full text and citation also at [components/rudder.md](/components/rudder.md#rudder-blade-stop-clonking-the-transom-corner-and-the-haul-up-rope-as-a-check); left to swing freely down, his own blade does clonk the transom corner but then moves away enough to allow the tiller's full swing; has never examined what angle it settles at; the haul-up rope can constrain it to hang at any shallower angle. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[214] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4922 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-02-02) — "Re: New guy question about interior finish": full text and citation also at [construction/epoxy-coating-technique.md](/construction/epoxy-coating-technique.md#interior-finish-bare-wood-epoxy-varnish-cetol-or-paint); epoxy-coated all his woodwork, then painted the sun-exposed bits, a job he now regrets since the paint has peeled steadily ever since; four years of UK sun has done no obvious damage to the exposed epoxy between the islands of still-adhered paint; suspects the "sunlight degrades epoxy" warning was calibrated to Florida sun; judges the epoxy coating a good, hard-wearing surface. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[215] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4930 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-02-03) — "Re: New guy question about interior finish": full text and citation also at [construction/epoxy-coating-technique.md](/construction/epoxy-coating-technique.md#interior-finish-bare-wood-epoxy-varnish-cetol-or-paint); the paint failure was entirely his own fault — an expensive primer specifically designed to stick to epoxy, which has worked well elsewhere, was applied in the middle of winter, and only afterward did the tin's own label turn out to say it should not be applied below 6°C; would not bother painting the inside if building again; asks what the UV-filtering properties of Lexan are. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[216] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 4932 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-02-04) — "Re: New guy question about interior finish": full text and citation also at [construction/epoxy-coating-technique.md](/construction/epoxy-coating-technique.md#interior-finish-bare-wood-epoxy-varnish-cetol-or-paint); with tinted Lexan already providing UV protection, there is even less need to paint the inside of a Paradox — the only time the sun can get at it is while actually sailing, and then, depending on the sailor's size, only what gets past the person plugging the hatch opening. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[217] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5005 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-18) — "Re: Cost of Build - Paradox": full text and citation also at [construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md](/construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md#a-2008-recurrence-the-cost-gap-between-a-top-job-and-an-economy-build); Matt Layden's plans specify exterior grade; hopes the better material will bring greater longevity with less maintenance; "What do they say about a hap'orth of tar?" Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[218] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5010 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-26) — "Re: paradox plans": full text and citation also at [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#a-second-march-2008-recurrence-and-delivery-time-data-points); suggests the distributor may simply be away on holiday; asks where the newcomer is based. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[219] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5012 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-03-27) — "Re: paradox plans": full text and citation also at [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#a-second-march-2008-recurrence-and-delivery-time-data-points); not more than a couple of weeks, as best remembered; if the distributor isn't answering e-mail he is almost certainly away somewhere. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[220] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5233 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-23) — "Re: Storing gas": full text and citation also at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#a-side-mounted-motor-and-fuel-sponson-considered-and-rejected-a-three-position-transom-bracket); adds photographs to the group's photo section — captioned "in the middle of the Atlantic" — showing Bill Serjeant refuelling, noting the lack of a safety harness and the engine left running and in gear. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[221] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5235 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-23) — "Re: newbie...just ordered plans": full text and citation also at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#further-field-experience-with-a-side-mounted-outboard); welcomes Rodney Cannon aboard; uses a 28 lb-thrust Minn Kota 12-volt motor, pushing "Little Jim" at 3 knots on about 30 amps or 2 knots on about 15 amps. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[222] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5254 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-24) — "Re: Alternative auxiliary power...": full text and citation also at [operations/sculling-and-yuloh-technique.md](/operations/sculling-and-yuloh-technique.md#performance) and [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#a-calorie-based-case-for-human-power-and-its-real-world-limits); confirms rowing (sculling) "Little Jim" is a low-energy exercise even for a self-described non-athlete — a 3-mile session averaging just under 2 knots, longest continuous session 4 hours with room to continue, but going fast tires a rower quickly. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[223] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5283 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-26) — "Re: Alternative auxiliary power...": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#a-deliberate-no-motor-choice-and-the-case-for-leaving-it-at-home) and [boats/faith.md](/boats/faith.md#a-tide-and-wind-case-for-the-outboard-on-the-blackwater); carries no petrol fallback, deliberately, to force himself to learn sail and yuloh technique properly; recalls Faith's owner's own steep Paradox learning curve, tied to reaching for the engine whenever conditions turned hard on the wind. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[224] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5267 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-25) — "Re: Alternative auxiliary power...": full text and citation also at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#further-field-experience-with-a-side-mounted-outboard); when he first asked about a motor for "Little Jim," advice called for at least 54 lb of thrust for a boat that heavy; a dealer let him try a 28 lb-thrust motor instead, which proved fine, giving a top speed of 3 knots. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[225] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5269 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-25) — "Re: Alternative auxiliary power...": full text and citation also at [operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md](/operations/auxiliary-outboard-power.md#further-field-experience-with-a-side-mounted-outboard); an 80Ah AGM deep-cycle battery, charged at home before and after each trip, runs the echosounder, GPS, VHF, and phone charging underway; after a 3-week cruise it was still about 80% charged; relies on the yuloh rather than the motor except at one narrow-river launch site. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[226] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5272 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-06-25) — "Re: Alternative auxiliary power...": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#building-the-yuloh-itself-blade-curvature-and-the-pivot-boltsocket); moved his own pivot hole about 150mm nearer the handle after finding the lower gearing gave too little purchase; settled on boring a worn socket oversized, filling with epoxy and fibres, and re-drilling to fit the ball, good for another season or two; lubricates the socket with molten candle wax to stop it squeaking. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[227] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5352 (openboat / "Al", `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-08-08) — "Re: It begins": full text and citation also at [boats/wizard-spawn.md](/boats/wizard-spawn.md#encouragement-from-established-builders); restates his own roughly 18-month rule of thumb for a typical build; a builder genuinely trying for a near-term launch has effectively solved the question of whether to build a practice boat first, since there will be no time left over for one, or for much sailing either. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[228] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5357 (openboat / "Al", `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-08-09) — "Re: It begins": full text and citation also at [boats/wizard-spawn.md](/boats/wizard-spawn.md#starting-on-the-beams-jigs-sizing-confusion-and-a-lesson-in-plywood), [boats/faith.md](/boats/faith.md#a-laminated-hatch-beam-that-delaminated-in-the-ply-itself), and [construction/fastening-and-gluing.md](/construction/fastening-and-gluing.md#fastening-laminated-ply-panels-a-delamination-caution-from-experience); repeats, to a new builder, the caution that a laminated plywood beam can delaminate within the plywood itself, independent of the epoxy bond — probably not a problem for beams bonded to bulkheads, but may affect free-standing ones. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[229] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5493 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-07) — "Re: p&s huh?": full text and citation also at [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#reading-the-plans-a-first-time-builders-backlog-of-questions); on the east side of the pond, "IN THE CAN" can be appended to indicate the shape of a buoy. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[230] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 5616 (openboat / alopenboat / Alastair Law — `From:` display name blank on this message, `paradoxbuilders`, 2008-09-29) — "Re: To Hawaii?": full text and citation also at [design/blue-water-suitability.md](/design/blue-water-suitability.md#a-2008-marina-del-rey-to-honolulu-plan-and-the-answer-it-drew); judges the boat itself would have no problem with the trip given the right conditions, the skipper being the weak link; the fact of asking the question suggests not yet being ready; recommends building the boat, making progressively longer cruises, and reading firsthand accounts of long ocean crossings in small boats, most of which describe a miserable time of it. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[231] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6052 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, c. 2009-05-06) — "Re: Matt Layden Interview": full text and citation also at [chine-runners/how-they-work.md](/chine-runners/how-they-work.md#a-rockercrescent-misconception-and-the-multihull-question-restated); not itself present in the archive as its own file — a genuine gap, confirmed absent, recovered only via a direct quotation in a later reply (message 6058); thanks Pete Martin for pointing out the yrvind.com extract of the 2009 *Small Craft Advisor* interview, not having previously appreciated what a big part the rudder played; glad his own interpretation of how it all works was not too far out; restates his assertion that chine runners will not work on a multihull, though vortex generators may. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England" (quoted in full within Andres Espino's reply, message 6058).
[232] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6297 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-04) — "Re: Chine Runners": full text and citation also at [chine-runners/how-they-work.md](/chine-runners/how-they-work.md#a-december-2009-debate-the-triloboat-16s-negative-result-and-matts-own-explanation-restated); from reading what Matt has said, the lateral resistance comes from the heeled hull behaving as a lifting body, with the chine runners "just the icing on the cake," so a hull without the right underwater profile under sail gains nothing from adding chine runners. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[233] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6314 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-05) — "Re: installing deck panels": full text and citation also at [construction/deck-and-cabin-beams.md](/construction/deck-and-cabin-beams.md#citations); nailed his own deck panels the same way as the rest of the boat, hammering the heads down flush and later glassing over them with light cloth and painting, without fairing them in; considers this a good thing, since the fastener positions stay identifiable under the paint and can be avoided later when screwing anything else to the boat. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[234] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6322 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-05) — "Re: Minimalist sail-inside designs": full text and citation also at [design/ideas/scaled-down-idea.md](/design/ideas/scaled-down-idea.md#don-elliotts-own-flush-deck-forward-design-and-the-fate-of-his-archive); not convinced about a forward-facing cabin opening — in his own experience with Little Jim, that is exactly where the cold wind and colder water come from. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[235] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6324 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2009-12-05) — "Re: Minimalist sail-inside designs": full text and citation also at [design/ideas/scaled-down-idea.md](/design/ideas/scaled-down-idea.md#don-elliotts-own-flush-deck-forward-design-and-the-fate-of-his-archive); with a flush deck, a sliding canopy proposed in the same thread would only need to be slid off to put the hull on a car roof. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[236] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6675 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2010-10-02) — "Re: Paradox history and capabilities": full text and citation also at [design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md](/design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md#a-2010-proposal-for-a-bolt-on-keel-declined); Paradox is already self-righting and self-rescuing, more so than the Micro; a keel would detract from her performance. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[237] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6679 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2010-10-03) — "Re: Paradox history and capabilities": full text and citation also at [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#beale-park-thames-boat-show-and-a-righting-moment-measurement-june-2009); clarifies the Beale Park test conditions — forgot to lower the rudder, reasoning real knockdown-risk weather would already have him retreated below with the hatch shut; was sprawled against the side bins rather than sitting on the bottom, and deliberately shifted position to check the effect, which made no noticeable difference to the masthead load; nothing had been displaced from its stowed position afterward, down to an unlit kettle still on the stove. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[238] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6705 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2010-10-27) — "Re: Trailer": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#al-laws-own-launching-ramp-trailer-and-a-return-to-the-weight-caution-october-2010); could find no commercial trailer meeting his needs so designed his own, with pictures on his website; allows single-handed launch/recovery with little physical effort; the drawback is needing a proper slipway reachable by car; docile on the road behind his 2-litre diesel car except on steep slipways; the main design requirement was keeping the trailer wheels dry, so the trailer itself acts as a launching ramp putting the boat into water deep enough to float, which puts considerable upward force on the towball. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[239] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6716 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2010-11-13) — "Re: mast wiring": full text and citation also at [construction/electrical-wiring.md](/construction/electrical-wiring.md#a-further-november-2010-recurrence-and-a-caution-against-an-exposed-mast-base-connector); embedded a waterproof socket into the mast just above the solid base, the plug inserted from inside the cabin by reaching in through the vent box; others instead bring the wire out there with a short lead and a socket, which can be a pain when inserting the mast; could not find a light with a low enough profile to let the mast still fit inside the hull, so made his own. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[240] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6722 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Al, `paradoxbuilders`, 2010-11-13) — "Re: mast wiring": full text and citation also at [construction/electrical-wiring.md](/construction/electrical-wiring.md#a-2010-hindsight-verdict-sector-overlap-and-a-case-for-skipping-the-tricolour-on-a-boat-this-size); links his own navlight page (`www.little.jim.freeuk.com/navlight/navlight_1.htm`); the bottom ring is 6 white LEDs, the top a mix of red, green, and white LEDs; judges the mix not very good given the large color overlap between sectors, and would fit only white (legal under COLREGs for a boat this size) if doing it again; strings the LEDs in series and regulates the voltage with a simple resistor, though a proper voltage regulator would probably work better. Signed "Al, Yeovil, England."
[241] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6754 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-01-02) — "Re: built in toilets": full text and citation also at [operations/head-and-sanitation.md](/operations/head-and-sanitation.md#a-further-2010-recurrence-the-great-lakes-regulatory-case-an-unsuitable-factory-head-and-a-wag-bag-recommendation); has seen a port-a-pottie carried in a Paradox's forward stateroom, needing to be pulled to the hatch each time it was used — not very convenient; if he could no longer use "bucket and chuck it," would turn to a commercial Wag Bag kit (`cleanwaste.com`). Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[242] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6812 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-02-17) — "Re: Chine logs": full text and citation also at [construction/chine-log-vs-taped-seam.md](/construction/chine-log-vs-taped-seam.md#a-2011-recurrence-two-more-builders-firsthand-installation-accounts); considers the chine logs the most physically demanding part of his own build; used a single, unlaminated piece rather than the plans' suggested lamination; a dry test-fit without glue left it under enough stored energy that he judged removing it too risky, so forced epoxy in around it in place instead; recommends laminating a number of strips in place, or steaming and pre-setting the timber's curve before fitting; the bottom, once fitted, ties the structure together regardless. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[243] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6828 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-03-17) — "Re: Small Fry rounds Cape Leeuwin": full text and citation also at [components/vent-baffle.md](/components/vent-baffle.md#citations); an even simpler fix than a fellow builder's rudder-hole boot is a disc of rubber on the tiller aft of the transom, which allows air to pass through from inside but, when a wave slaps the back, presses up against the transom and acts as a seal — "a bit like the mushroom valve in your inflatable air tubes, but a much looser fit." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[244] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6830 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-03-17) — "Re: Small Fry rounds Cape Leeuwin": full text and citation also at [components/vent-baffle.md](/components/vent-baffle.md#citations); replying to a question whether the tiller hole is integral to ventilation, confirms that is why the loose disc works so well, since in normal conditions it doesn't close off the hole; it doesn't need to be watertight, only needs to reduce the velocity of the water so it doesn't reach the top of the baffle. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[245] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6835 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-03-18) — "Re: Small Fry rounds Cape Leeuwin": full text and citation also at [components/vent-baffle.md](/components/vent-baffle.md#a-2011-warm-water-ventilation-question-and-a-six-year-dry-record) and [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#a-navigational-error-into-a-tide-race-swamping-avoided); answers a ventilation question about warmer waters, noting the boat was designed for the Florida coast and an air scoop could be added; a mugful of water has come in via the tiller opening only once in six years, while beached; recommends a door-mat inside the hatch; retells his early tide-race mishap, "when I didn't have any idea what I was doing," seasick throughout but not a drop came in. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[246] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6837 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-03-18) — "Re: Small Fry rounds Cape Leeuwin": full text and citation also at [operations/sailing-characteristics.md](/operations/sailing-characteristics.md#a-one-tack-fluttering-puzzle-and-a-contrary-experience); has always felt he can point higher on the port tack too; speculates it may be because the point at which the sail starts to stall is more clearly defined on that tack. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[247] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6841 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-03-23) — "Re: How do you sit/balance in your Paradox when it heels?": full text and citation also at [components/interior.md](/components/interior.md#quintessential-seat); tends to lounge into the leeward corner; on a long coastal passage, sometimes relieves the aches by standing and leaning on the cabin top; keeps a selection of cushions, swapped around roughly every hour. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[248] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6891 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-05-25) — "Re: Johanna": full text and citation also at [boats/johanna.md](/boats/johanna.md#citations) and [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#citations); "Been there, done that"; often gets blisters at the start of the season, and carries a pair of sailing gloves put on as needed until his hands harden up. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[249] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6887 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-05-25) — "Re: New Paradox Odyssey is launched": full text and citation also at [boats/odyssey.md](/boats/odyssey.md#citations) and [design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md](/design/self-righting-and-capsize-recovery.md#a-2011-comparison-a-heavier-hanging-load-needed-to-heel-odyssey-than-little-jim); "Very nice, and very pretty"; wonders whether Fadeev is a lot heavier than him, or needs more ballast, since he cannot get his own Paradox anywhere near going over by hanging out on the mast. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[250] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 6895 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-05-29) — "Re: Living ability for 2": full text and citation also at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#a-2011-answer-canvas-roof-and-a-second-seat-for-occasional-day-sailing-two-up); suggests replacing the plywood cabin roof and hatch with canvas, with a second seat added at bulkhead 3 so a guest could sit forward of the skipper once the roof is removed; this would reduce the boat's ultimate seaworthiness but would still likely be safer than any open dinghy. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[251] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7000 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-07-19) — "Re: begionning trailer Re: Trailers": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#faiths-trailer-failure-and-a-rebuild-from-a-scrapped-van-axle-july-2011); his own bearing buddies, bought years earlier, came with adaptor rings that seemed to fit almost anything; after later changing suspension units the adaptors no longer fitted, so he sawed the end off the new hubs' bearing caps and brazed the buddies directly into them instead. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[252] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7030 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-07-29) — "Re: Update on ENDEAVOUR": full text and citation also at [boats/endeavour.md](/boats/endeavour.md#citations) and [people/greg-lockhart.md](/people/greg-lockhart.md#citations); "If it ain't broke, why fix it? It would be easy to change Paradox, a lot harder to improve it." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[253] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7033 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-07-30) — "Re: aluminium paradox": full text and citation also at [design/ideas/aluminum-construction-idea.md](/design/ideas/aluminum-construction-idea.md#a-2011-recurrence-serge-testas-precedent-and-a-fuller-construction-proposal); "Well, try a small wooden barrel. These days a plastic drinks bottle would probably survive better, especially if it (can or bottle) gets run over. I am not convinced that makes either of them a good design for a sailing craft, however." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[254] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7039 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-08-07) — "Re: roller furling system/ bronze rod": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#citations); "A strong vice, a long lever and a big hammer." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[255] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7085 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2011-09-10) — "Re: Boom drilling": full text and citation also at [construction/boom-end-tack-line-holes.md](/construction/boom-end-tack-line-holes.md#drilling-the-tack-end-for-the-swivel-rod-bearing-and-a-further-tang-bolt-caution) and [components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md](/components/mainsheet-outboard-fouling.md#boom-end-splitting-at-the-mainsheet-tang-bolt-and-a-construction-fix); clamped the boom horizontally in a vice and drilled the tack-end hole freehand, oversize, then epoxied in a length of 15mm copper pipe (capped at its end) to give a good bearing surface for the stainless swivel rod; at the boom's other end, restated the drilled-oversize/epoxy-filled/re-tapped tang-bolt fix, cautioning it should be built stronger than seems necessary since several builders have had the tang bend during use, preventing the rig from being reefed. Signed "Alastair."
[256] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7180 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-02-25) — "Re: Fafnir or Paradox": full text and citation also at [similar-boat-designs/comparisons.md](/similar-boat-designs/comparisons.md#john-welsfords-fafnir); it depends on the coastline — his own Paradox works for coastal cruising on the south coast of England where a Fafnir, drawing 2 ft 7 in, would not, unable to cross the bar into some of the smaller rivers, awkward getting ashore, and a challenge on and off a trailer; Paradox, by contrast, often lets him run the bow ashore and step off dry-shod; a "nice boat" for staying at sea, though. Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[257] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7227 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-03-25) — "Re: Going from 2d plans to 3d plans": full text and citation also at [references/building-plans.md](/references/building-plans.md#a-march-2012-proposal-3d-cad-models-to-supplement-the-paper-plans); before starting his own build, "assembled" a Paradox using the 3D CAD software he used at work, well worth the effort, since it sorted out a lot of the queries he had about how the parts fitted together. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[258] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7247 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-06-01) — "Re: Sitting in Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/interior.md](/components/interior.md#a-beanbag-chair-a-footwell-or-a-low-bench-for-hatch-closed-sitting); either a beanbag or a footwell should work; spends little time shut below even in British weather; rotates through five cushions of different construction roughly every hour; restates the notched-seat-support/sloping-backrest technique rather than stowing the seat flat against bulkhead 4. Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[259] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7311 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-09-04) — "Re: Has anyone built a 16.5' Paradox?": full text and citation also at [design/larger-paradox.md](/design/larger-paradox.md#a-2012-recurrence-the-165-ft-document-revisited-and-two-fit-driven-size-questions); "Remember that when you are on the water the boat is moving about all the time, not just sitting there like a mock-up."
[260] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7313 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-09-04) — "Re: Has anyone built a 16.5' Paradox?": full text and citation also at [design/larger-paradox.md](/design/larger-paradox.md#a-2012-recurrence-the-165-ft-document-revisited-and-two-fit-driven-size-questions); notes a shopping mall would more likely lend a coin-operated kiddie ride than let it run six hours on coins; offers an alternative comfortable sailing position from his own long-passage practice — standing up and resting the elbows on the hatch, or hanging over the side and watching the water go past. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[261] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7318 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-09-08) — "Re: weights and thicknesses": full text and citation also at [construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md](/construction/plywood-grades-and-foam-flotation.md#a-2012-recurrence-the-9mm-substitution-question-restated-and-a-structural-weightballast-tradeoff); "If you put less weight in the boat structure you will need to add more lead ballast. It is better to have the weight where it will do most good." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[262] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7319 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-09-08) — "Re: Paradox weight capacity": full text and citation also at [design/weight-and-ballast.md](/design/weight-and-ballast.md#a-2012-cargo-capacity-question-and-a-two-extra-passenger-data-point); displacement in sailing trim (skipper aboard) is 640 kg (1,410 lb), of which, with little other gear aboard, 180 kg is lead ballast and 70 kg is water ballast, leaving in theory about 180 kg for stores, anchors, bicycles, batteries, etc.; in practice has trimmed the boat for himself and, on occasion, carried two other people without noticing any significant change in sailing performance, though it was a bit crowded and they didn't sail far. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[263] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7321 (OpenBoat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2012-09-08) — "Re: Paradox weight capacity": full text and citation also at [design/weight-and-ballast.md](/design/weight-and-ballast.md#a-2012-cargo-capacity-question-and-a-two-extra-passenger-data-point); "Well, like I indicated, add whatever weight you want and balance it by removing lead ballast to bring it back to the correct displacement." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[264] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7830 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2013-10-15) — "Re: Scout prepares for the Bosunator Challenge": full text and citation also at [components/yuloh.md](/components/yuloh.md#ball-joint-versus-oarlock-and-pivot-socket-wear-over-time) and [boats/scout.md](/boats/scout.md#the-bosunator-challenge-a-recurring-yuloh-socket-problem-and-a-fourth-shakedown-video); his own pivot is seated in thickened epoxy — a hole a few mm wider than the ball, filled with thickened epoxy, then re-drilled to the ball's own size, its depth adjustable at will by filling and re-drilling again; the socket eventually wears and needs refurbishing every 4 or 5 years; confirms the design already calls for the pivot pin to be offset. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[265] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 7891 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2013-12-11) — "Re: Short video clips of Scout sailing today in 15-25": full text and citation also at [boats/scout.md](/boats/scout.md#citations), [operations/pointing-and-tacking-angles.md](/operations/pointing-and-tacking-angles.md#citations), and [boats/little-jim.md](/boats/little-jim.md#a-lee-shore-self-rescue-under-sail-alone-december-2013); finds a fellow owner's 90-95 degree tacking-angle estimate comforting, having guessed something similar himself; reckons about 20 degrees the optimum heel angle, and describes raising or lowering the sail about 6 inches partway through a tack to fine-tune an over- or under-reefed sail; recounts sailing off a lee shore under sail alone by dropping the rudder onto the mud and using the boarding step to get aboard once clear. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[266] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8005 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2014-01-28) — "Re: First try with my Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#a-further-boom-end-refinement-tang-spacing-and-a-high-topping-lift-join-january-2014) and [boats/ironsides.md](/boats/ironsides.md#a-first-sail-at-lake-amistad-texas-and-a-topping-liftfurling-snarl-january-2014); recounts an identical topping-lift/sheet knockdown of his own from early ownership, and the tang-spacing and topping-lift-join fix that ended it. Signed "Hoping for calm nights, Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[267] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8270 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-03-31) — "Re: capsize": replying to a fellow owner's "that's not sissy, that's sensible," argues you have to go beyond sensible sometimes just to recalibrate what is sensible, or you creep into bland; "fortunately every time I have gone beyond sensible and into stupid my Paradox has bailed me out." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[268] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8351 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-05-12) — "Re: Yard Part Build for Paradox": full text and citation also at [components/rig/index.md](/components/rig/index.md#yard-material-douglas-fir-remains-standard-aluminum-a-proven-alternative); used Douglas fir and has no complaints, still going strong after 13 years. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[269] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8403 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-07-07) — "Re: Paradox registry/census": full text and citation also at [references/builders-and-owners-census-2014.md](/references/builders-and-owners-census-2014.md#a-2015-proposal-for-a-group-maintained-update-and-a-short-lived-successor-registry) and [boats/enuf.md](/boats/enuf.md#citations); "Good work! A couple of amendments: Bill Sergeant (row 9) built Faith but subsequently sold her to Jim Hall, Shaftesbury UK. Derek Clark (row 7) built Enuf then sold her to Robert Beigler (row 12). He sold her to Bill Sergeant who renamed her Minnow and has since sold her to Richard Green. I also have vague recollections of: Petite Lady in New Zealand built, I think, by Hugh Miller but now owned by P D Benn. One built in Sweden by Kjell Karlsson. Raffles built by Mark Waters in Sacramento California." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[270] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8471 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-07-28) — "Re: Wood for Boat Question and opinions": full text and citation also at [components/rig/mast.md](/components/rig/mast.md#reading-the-mast-drawing-a-recurring-point-of-confusion); answering a beginner's mast-shaping question, sets out the build order — shape the pieces first, leaving the side pieces' forward edge oversized to help the next step; fit the tapered front and rear pieces to one side, checking the mast is not bent, made easy because the rear of the mast is straight; fit the top and bottom plugs, paint/seal the inside and fit any internal wiring; fasten on the other side; round off the corners. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[271] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8613 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-08-31) — "Re: Trunk Cabin vs Raised Deck Cabin": full text and citation also at [components/pilothouse.md](/components/pilothouse.md#trunk-cabin-versus-a-raised-deck-cabin); most of the space under the side deck is shelving and storage bins, so moving the windows out would not really increase interior space; has never been in any danger of hitting his head on the sides, usually lounging in the corner of the seat, and as the sitting position is near the centre of rotation there doesn't seem to be a great deal of relative motion; if two were aboard they would be unlikely to be sitting side-by-side, so the windows would be no closer than with only one. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[272] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8617 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-08-31) — "Re: Trunk Cabin vs Raised Deck Cabin": full text and citation also at [components/pilothouse.md](/components/pilothouse.md#trunk-cabin-versus-a-raised-deck-cabin); "I concur with all of that, especially the last paragraph." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[273] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8619 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-08-31) — "Re: Trunk Cabin vs Raised Deck Cabin": full text and citation also at [components/pilothouse.md](/components/pilothouse.md#trunk-cabin-versus-a-raised-deck-cabin); pushing the windows to the sheer line certainly wouldn't improve visibility; would miss the side window at the rear corner of the cabin, where he rests his shoulder in normal relaxed sailing mode; going forward would be more difficult and, with weight higher up, there may be a stability problem for heavier sailors; the neat sail stowage would also be lost; given the choice, would stick with the designed layout. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[274] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8624 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-09-01) — "Re: Trunk Cabin vs Raised Deck Cabin": full text and citation also at [operations/crew-size-and-fit.md](/operations/crew-size-and-fit.md#a-2015-sitting-height-question-and-a-builder-to-bes-own-trial); "I am 5'6" and have no problem sitting inside wearing a hat. Because the boat will probably be heeled (when sailing) it is necessary to duck down to look to leeward and sit up to look to windward. When sailing I am never sitting bolt upright but am lounging back so the sitting headroom is greater than might, at first, be expected." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[275] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) messages 8643/8645 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-09-10) — "Re: cruise write-up": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#dry-launching-onto-an-exposed-slipway-at-low-tide); asked why launching onto a dry slipway rather than into the water would anger anyone, answers, "It is a technique I often use"; the boatyard slipway used charges for its use, and the boat was launched onto the slipway rather than into the water because the tide had gone out, leaving acres of mud stretching nearly half a mile to navigable water, so no one else, certainly no powerboater, would want the slipway until the water returned; one reason for often using the technique is having the slipway entirely to himself, with no rush — drop the boat off the trailer, park the car and trailer, board, make coffee, read a book, and wait for the water. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[276] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8657 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-09-13) — "Re: cruise write-up": full text and citation also at [construction/chine-log-vs-taped-seam.md](/construction/chine-log-vs-taped-seam.md#a-2015-recurrence-a-water-bucket-jig-steaming-considered-and-a-broken-sheer-clamp); answering the same chine-log question again four years later, repeats the 2011 account essentially unchanged: fitted the chine logs as drawn; a dry fit first showed enough stored energy in the bent wood that removing it again for gluing seemed too risky, so it was left in place with epoxy forced in around it; would laminate them if building again. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[277] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8649 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-09-10) — "Re: cruise write-up": replying to a fellow correspondent's remark that building a Paradox having never seen one in person took real nerve — the same position Alastair had been in building the first one in Europe — offers his own dry alternative: "Or, perhaps, an ignorant one." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[278] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8703 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-11-05) — "Re: Copper Pipe Solid Fuel Heater": full text and citation also at [operations/cabin-heating.md](/operations/cabin-heating.md#a-november-2015-continuation-further-heater-detail-and-a-foam-insulation-contrast); as designed, Paradox has 2 in of foam lining the sides and deck for flotation, soundproofing against slapping halyards, and insulation; believes Faith was never fitted with this by her original builder, explaining the felt need for a heater there; has never felt the need for a heater himself, even when it snowed or he woke to frost on the deck on his first night afloat, relying mostly on body heat and, on a cold evening, a coffee made before bed. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[279] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8714 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-11-22) — "Re: Sailing in big seas": full text and citation also at [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#why-short-steep-seas-stall-windward-progress-even-when-reefed-2015) and [boats/thor.md](/boats/thor.md#a-heavy-weather-sail-a-boom-problem-and-a-coast-guard-tow-22-november-2015); "It is the waves that kill the speed in small boats like these. If you could have found a bit of flat water you would probably have made good windward progress. I try to be a gentleman and therefore resist sailing to windward." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[280] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8722 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-11-25) — "Re: sail shape": full text and citation also at [components/rig/mk2-sail.md](/components/rig/mk2-sail.md#a-yard-tapersail-cut-mismatch-and-a-longer-replacement-yard); when he had his own Mk2 sail made, gave the sailmaker both the yard and the boom to work from, who "did an excellent job" as a result (offers an old, badly cut Mk1 sail to anyone who wants it); "if your sailmaker doesn't know why you are waving a yard and boom about find another sailmaker." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[281] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8729 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2015-11-29) — "Re: Sail rigging": full text and citation also at [components/rig/furling.md](/components/rig/furling.md#alastairs-fuller-walkthrough-and-a-continuous-halyardfurling-line-innovation); to help with the masses-of-rope problem, uses the same rope for the halyard and the furling line — one end attached to the yard, the other to the furling drum; 12 m of rope leaves about 2 m (a doubled rope 1 m long) inside the cabin; a 2:1 purchase on the halyard would mean more rope needed while sailing, but still less than using two separate ropes. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[282] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8763 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2016-01-22) — "Re: Sail position": full text and citation also at [operations/sailing-characteristics.md](/operations/sailing-characteristics.md#a-one-tack-fluttering-puzzle-and-a-contrary-experience); suspects a centred sail would exacerbate the tacking problem rather than cure it; as built, the boat can usually be persuaded to fall off onto the starboard tack; it is not really a big problem, arising mainly when not concentrating; a bit of chop needs enough speed built up before tacking; in winds over Force 6 it is often better to wear round than to tack. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[283] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8811 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2016-04-12) — "Re: Trailer bunks": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#a-2016-recurrence-transverse-versus-longitudinal-bunks); a boat built to the plans doesn't need to worry about locating trailer support under specific bulkheads, since the structure is strong enough to take the load anywhere; his own trailer has 4 transverse rollers, 2 supporting the boat while travelling and 2 further aft letting it be winched on and off, so the wheels never need to go in the water at a slipway and the boat can be launched/recovered even on a dried-out beach; after 13 years he still hasn't got the brakes wet. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[284] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8817 (openboat / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2016-04-13) — "Re: Trailer bunks": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#a-2016-recurrence-transverse-versus-longitudinal-bunks); whatever the law states, manhandling the boat down the first slope without brakes would risk a runaway; his own design has no tipping mechanism, just a rigid (and therefore heavy) platform built so the trailer's back end lines up with the boat's own stern when properly stowed, meaning that with the wheels at the water's edge the trailer's back end sits over water deep enough to float the boat; the boat is so over-designed that damage to it while winching aboard is rarely a real consideration, and anyone winching a boat on should size the winch several sizes up from what would seem needed — his own is a 900 kg winch, whose gearing makes hauling the boat on easy. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[285] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8873 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2016-05-14) — "Re: Mast dimensions": full text and citation also at [components/rig/mast.md](/components/rig/mast.md#reading-the-mast-drawing-a-recurring-point-of-confusion) and [people/william-johnston.md](/people/william-johnston.md#rebuilding-the-mast-for-weight-may-2016); resolves the recurring sheet-12 mast-drawing confusion in full for a builder rebuilding his own mast — discounts the stray superfluous dimension arrows to the left of the centreline as a drafting error; reads the half-section to the right of the centreline (starboard, viewed from the stern) as 19 mm side cheek plus a 20 mm hole, doubling to 78 mm; reads the section to the left of the centreline (viewed from the port side) as 86 mm total width at deck level, 24 mm each for the front and rear infill pieces, 38 mm left for the hole; identifies "Section at Deck" as a half-section plan view of the starboard half at deck level; and gives the meaning of each of the four horizontal dimension-line figures in turn. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[286] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8889 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2016-06-08) — "Re: Storage space?": full text and citation also at [components/interior.md](/components/interior.md#citations); agrees with Jim — remove the dividers in the "forward stateroom." Stores an emergency change of clothes and wellington boots in the fore-peak; clothes, sleeping bag, air bed, an electric (outboard) motor, an inflatable tender, a battery charger, and a bicycle in the forward stateroom; a week's food and water under the port floor locker (usually about half full), with nibbles, crisps, and coffee in the starboard rear bin; tools, a spare anchor, chain, and rope in the starboard floor locker; wet-weather gear and wet shoes (several pairs) and spare gas cans in the forward side lockers; binoculars and a rubbish bin in the rear port side locker, otherwise mostly empty; a cooker, an anchor with chain and rope, mooring ropes, fenders, and an in-use water bottle in the stern locker; and gloves, papers, a coffee mug, a camera, a radio, and a torch on the side shelves. None of the lockers are full to capacity; doesn't feel short of storage space, and friends in more conventional dinghies are jealous of how much he has. Agrees that crossing an ocean in a Paradox is a silly idea — "What is the point in owning a boat like that if you don't put it on the ferry?" Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[287] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8908 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2016-06-20) — "Re: Storage space?": the only pipes to his own tanks are the vents and the deck-mounted fillers shown on the plans; has installed a submersible electric bilge pump into each ballast tank to empty them, and uses a roving electric bilge pump to fill them. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[288] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 8980 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2017-01-29) — "Re: Foredeck Hatch?": full text and citation also at [components/interior.md](/components/interior.md#forepeak-stowage-and-retrieval-a-2017-exchange); keeps a watertight tub in the forepeak packed with a complete change of clothing, a towel, and a space blanket for emergencies, left in place for the whole season; lines the cabin floor with rubber matting sold for garages and workshops; removed the forward storage area's internal partition, replacing it with a bungee-cord fence, and dropped its floor to stow a bicycle, opening the hatchway to match, which also lets feet go into the storage area for extra sleeping length; recalls a different boat's owner using cloth bags each fitted with a string, left protruding so the bag can be pulled back out. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[289] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9124 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2017-10-07) — "Re: Trailer": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#a-2017-recurrence-a-flatbed-conversion-a-restated-roller-design-and-a-carpeted-bunk-build); pictures of his own trailer at `www.little.jim.freeuk.com`; a series of horizontal rollers with a couple of vertical rollers to keep it reasonably centred, the front two horizontal rollers since removed for doing little; "somewhat over engineered," but has lasted 15 years without significant problems; can launch and recover from a slipway, or onto the ground, without putting the wheels in the water; recommends a winch several sizes bigger than seems necessary (his own rated at 950 kg) and a braked winch over the standard ratchet type; a braked trailer makes manhandling much easier. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[290] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9231 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-02-02) — "Re: Building the mast": full text and citation also at [components/rig/mast.md](/components/rig/mast.md#reading-the-mast-drawing-a-recurring-point-of-confusion); the main problem with the mast drawing is that Matt tried to squeeze it into a small space; if you want to really clarify the mast drawing, separate the 3 parts into 3 separate drawings. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[291] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9230 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-02-02) — "Re: Plexiglass View Dome?": full text and citation also at [components/hatch.md](/components/hatch.md#the-plexiglass-dome-question-recurs-2018); "It is not really necessary as there is a good view through the windows and it might be difficult to get it to line up with the designed sitting position." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[292] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9242 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-02-22) — "Re: Port Macquarie to Lord Howe Island": full text and citation also at [design/blue-water-suitability.md](/design/blue-water-suitability.md#a-2018-test-case-370-nautical-miles-of-open-pacific-to-lord-howe-island); a could/would/should distinction on a newcomer's 370 nm open-Pacific passage question — many less seaworthy boats have made similar trips, so the boat could likely do it, but whether it would succeed depends on conditions, and whether it should be attempted is not his place to say; often asked whether he could sail "Little Jim" across the English Channel to France — could, but what's the use of a boat like this if you don't put it on the ferry; finds crossing open water out of sight of land very tedious, preferring to sail the French coast instead. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[293] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9421 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-10-04) — "Re: Paradox Trailer Bolt": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#removing-the-boat-from-the-trailer-entirely-for-storage-or-a-beach-launch-october-2018); frequently drops and recovers his own Paradox onto the hard; his trailer consists of a number of horizontal rollers, fairly low-slung and overhanging the wheels quite a bit; launching is pushing the boat off the trailer until it tips so the rudder touches the ground, then driving forward until the boat is left behind; recovery needs rollers at the trailer's rear, which run on the ground once the trailer is tipped up, presenting its rear under the bow for the winch to pull the trailer in underneath the boat; carries a couple of boards to lay down for the rollers if the ground is soft or too rough. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[294] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9425 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-10-05) — "Re: Removing boat from trailer (was Paradox Trailer Bolt)": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#removing-the-boat-from-the-trailer-entirely-for-storage-or-a-beach-launch-october-2018); when hauling a boat down a beach, finds it easier to proceed stern-first, since the rudder can be a problem going forward; with no special equipment, it is possible to "walk" the boat sideways — slow, but progress is possible; is a firm believer in a well-designed trailer matched to the boat, rather than making do with whatever trailer happens to be at hand; spent quite a lot having a purpose-built trailer made and has never regretted it; in his late 60s can launch and recover his own Paradox single-handed without breaking into a sweat in about 15 minutes, meaning he is much more likely to go out for a short sail, and ends up sailing much more as a result. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[295] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9429 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-10-05) — "Re: Removing boat from trailer (was Paradox Trailer Bolt)": full text and citation also at [operations/trailering.md](/operations/trailering.md#removing-the-boat-from-the-trailer-entirely-for-storage-or-a-beach-launch-october-2018); points to his own trailer page (`little.jim.freeuk.com`, "trailer"); interested to hear how the inflatable rollers work out, having wondered about something similar himself; notes one possible advantage — with the boat on the ground, deflated rollers could be inserted underneath and then inflated, lifting the boat off the ground; bearing buddies are a great invention, used in the past without a bearing problem, but not fitted to his own Paradox trailer since its wheels never go into the water. Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[296] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) message 9449 (paradox / "alopenboat" / Alastair Law, `paradoxbuilders`, 2018-10-25) — "Re: The day things went wrong": full text and citation also at [operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md](/operations/heavy-weather-tactics.md#departing-through-breaking-surf-an-outboard-sprint-or-a-kedge-and-haul-and-riding-waves-bow-on); "I can report that, as long as you keep the bow square to the waves a Paradox will ride very comfortably through waves breaking considerably higher than the sheer, with no water coming over the deck. (I was very impressed). Let the bow fall off, however......." Signed "Alastair Law, Yeovil, England."
[297] [Paradox builders mailing list](/references/mailing-list.md) ("alastair" <paradox@...>, `paradoxsailboat`, groups.io, 2023-01-26) — "Re: Lifelines": full text and citation also at [operations/personal-safety-gear.md](/operations/personal-safety-gear.md#lifelines-and-pulpits-a-permanent-bow-to-transom-line-instead); has a line each side permanently attached at the bow and secured near the transom — an instant bow rope for tying to a pontoon, a line to attach to the anchor rope so he can anchor from the bow without leaving the cockpit, and a line to attach a harness to on the unlikely occasion of going on deck at sea, not needed in the last 20 years; would not go forward with the sail up, since the boom would do its best to throw him overboard; the mast is his main handhold if he does reach the foredeck. Sent from the `paradox@as6jg.freeuk.com` account (`X-Originating-Location: Yeovil, England, GB`), confirming the established Alastair Law identity. No message number survives for this groups.io-era post.
